Skillshare help

This guide holds Skillshare’s own help, gathered on 12 October 2026 from its own listing, with every article’s title and text. Skillshare writes for students and for teachers in one help centre, and both are here under Skillshare’s own categories, so a student can skip the teaching ones. It includes Skillshare’s keyboard shortcuts.

The help text belongs to its publisher and is gathered here unchanged for accessible reading. This guide’s own arrangement and wording are available under CC BY-SA 4.0.

Basics of Teaching

Complete List of Categories on Skillshare

Skillshare is a place where people come to learn from other people. Our classes are taught by humans sharing skills, creative practices, and their own unique perspectives - which is what continues to make the Skillshare community so valuable.

As Skillshare evolves, we’re focusing our efforts on the creative disciplines where students are most engaged and where we see the strongest opportunities for teachers to build meaningful connections with their audiences. While you may still find classes across a broader range of subjects, our current category strategy is centered on a curated set of creative fields.

The Top Level categories currently open to new teachers are:

  • AI & Innovation

  • Animation & 3D

  • Art & Illustration

  • Crafts & DIY

  • Creative Career

  • Creativity & Inspiration

  • Design

  • Film & video

  • Photography Additional categories remain available on Skillshare for students to explore, but we are not currently accepting new teachers in those areas. Those categories are:

  • Development

  • Home & Lifestyle

  • Marketing & Business

  • Music & Audio

  • Personal Development

  • Productivity

  • Writing & Publishing Each top level category includes subcategories that ladder up into it. Please note that subcategories can appear under multiple categories if relevant to multiple disciplines (such as Visual Effects appearing under both Film & Video and Animation & 3D).

If you’re unsure where your class fits, reach out to our team at teach@skillshare.com. We’re happy to help!

Get Started With Teaching

Whether you’re creating your first Skillshare class or preparing your next one, this guide walks you through the entire class creation process—from applying to teach to publishing and engaging with your students.

Along the way, you’ll find links to Help Center resources that explain each step in more detail.

  • Continue Your Teaching Journey

Get Ready

Before creating your first class, you’ll need to apply and be approved to teach on Skillshare. Review How do I apply to teach a class on Skillshare? to learn more about the application process.

We also recommend familiarizing yourself with how teaching works on Skillshare before you begin by exploring the Basics of Teaching section of the Teacher Help Center. Be sure to also check through our Teaching Policies to understand teaching requirements and what’s permitted on the platform.

For a step-by-step walkthrough on creating and publishing your first class, check out our class, Teach on Skillshare: Plan, Publish, and Promote an Engaging Class.

Plan Your Class

Planning is the first and most important part of creating your Skillshare class. This is when you decide on your topic, come up with an engaging class project, and get your thoughts organized. Great planning makes production much easier (and faster). If you put the time into planning at the start, the whole class creation process will go much more smoothly!

  • Class Planning Template
  • Experiment with Class Formats
  • Choose Your Class Topic
  • Give Your Class a Title
  • Write Your Class Description
  • Craft Your Class Project
  • Outline Your Class

Understand Class Length & Timeline

How long should my class be?

Most Skillshare classes include:

  • 20–60 minutes of pre-recorded video

  • Lessons broken into 2–8 minute videos Short-form classes

  • 2–15 minutes of video

  • Include a class project

  • Don’t require an introduction or outro Standard classes

  • More than 15 minutes of video

  • Include a standalone introduction, several lessons, a class project, and an outro While classes can certainly be longer, focus on creating the amount of content your topic needs—not simply making the class longer. Well-paced, high-quality classes tend to keep students engaged and encourage more minutes watched.

New classes cannot exceed 12 hours in duration.

Film & Edit Your Class

Once your outline is complete, it’s time to record your lessons.

If you’ve never produced video content before, this aspect of creating a class might feel intimidating. But many successful Skillshare teachers create stunning content, at home, all on their own. This section includes articles to help you prepare your space for filming, get confident on camera, film your content, and edit your footage into polished lessons.

  • Set Up Your Space

  • Teach With Confidence
  • Film Your Class
  • Edit Your Class
  • What settings should I use for recording and exporting my videos?

Upload Your Class

When your videos and project materials are ready, upload your content and prepare your class for publishing.

When you’re ready to publish, visit the Upload Your Class Content section of the Teacher Help Center for step-by-step guidance on uploading, strategies for dealing with uploading errors, and optimizing your class page.

Promote Your Class

A polished class page helps students discover your class. We recommend understanding marketing and merchandising before you launch to maximize your reach with potential students.

To learn how to showcase your class, strengthen your teacher profile, and share your work with a wider audience, explore the Promote Your Teaching section of the Teacher Help Center.

Launch Your Class

We recommend going through the Publishing Checklist and reviewing our Teaching Policies prior to publishing.

Most common mistakes teachers make that result in class closures are:

  • not having adequate A/V quality
  • not including a project
  • not titling each lesson
  • not having a standalone introduction lesson Once you’ve gone through the checklist and confirmed your class is good to go, push submit to publish your class!

New classes become available immediately after submission, while our Moderation team reviews each new class within 72 hours. For more information, see Class Moderation.

Engage Your Students

Publishing your class is just the beginning. Active teachers build stronger communities by interacting with students and encouraging participation.

Learn how to build an engaged learning community by visiting the Engage Your Students section of the Teacher Help Center, where you’ll find tips for facilitating discussions and providing feedback on student projects.

Continue Your Teaching Journey

As you continue teaching, you can grow your audience, strengthen your brand, and update your classes over time.

As you continue teaching, visit the Continue Your Teaching section of the Teacher Help Center for resources on improving your classes, growing your audience, building your brand, and making the most of your teaching experience on Skillshare.

Still need help?

If you have any questions, please do not hesitate to email us at teach@skillshare.com. We’re happy to help!

How do I access Creator Hub?

Creator Hub is your all-in-one space for managing everything you offer on Skillshare.

To access Creator Hub, log in to Skillshare with your teaching credentials and click Teach in the top navigation bar — you’ll be automatically redirected. If you’re not yet approved to teach on Skillshare, you’ll need to go through our teacher sign up process first — read more about that here.

You can also access Creator Hub by navigating to creator.skillshare.com.

Right now, Creator Hub offers immediate access to all of the tools you need to manage your content across Skillshare, divided into three main sections — Classes, Shop, and your general settings — plus a dedicated space for managing notifications across Skillshare.

Classes

Manage your Skillshare class content, view class performance, and review referral history.

  • Dashboard: Main page for your Skillshare class content and referrals, with quick links to your most frequently used tools and resources to support your work as a teacher and creator.
  • Create a Class: Create a new class draft.
  • My Classes: See all of your published, draft, and closed classes on one page.
  • Teacher Stats: An overview of performance and earnings across all of your classes, as well as a link to your Teacher Stats dashboard. Read Where can I find information on my performance and earnings on Skillshare? to learn more.
  • Referral Stats: Get detailed information on your referrals and referral payouts.
  • Engage with Students: A shortcut to posting a general discussion to members that follow your Skillshare profile.

Shop

Tools to grow your reach and income on Skillshare.

  • 1-on-1 Sessions: Offer personalized coaching or advice through private video calls. You can create new sessions or manage upcoming bookings. Review all articles on how to get started at Host Skillshare 1-on-1 Sessions.
  • Digital Products: create and manage templates, tools, or resources you’ve created to sell on Skillshare — and earn passive income while sharing your expertise. Learn how to get started with selling with Sell Digital Products.
  • Discount Codes: Create and manage custom discount codes to promote your digital products or 1-on-1 Sessions.
  • Messages: Send personalized follow-ups or thank-you notes to students who’ve booked a 1-on-1 with you.

General Settings

Everything related to your teacher profile, email settings, and payments.

  • Edit Profile: Update your name, bio, profile image, and social links to students across Skillshare.
  • Payment: Manage your payout settings and view your earnings from classes and Shop offerings.
  • Settings: Adjust your account settings, preferences, integrations, and notification settings.
  • Help: Access the Help Center for support articles.
  • Switch to Learn: Toggle back to the student view of Skillshare to browse and take classes.

Notifications

See updates from across Skillshare all in one place, including class project uploads, reviews, messages, 1-on-1 bookings, and digital product sales. Use the filters to zero in on exactly what you need.

Want to know more about our plans for Creator Hub? Read all about it in our announcement, Coming Soon! Creator Hub: Your New Home for Managing Content on Skillshare.

How do I apply to teach a class on Skillshare?

Skillshare’s application process for first-time teachers aims to create a high-quality learning experience for our member community and a positive experience for our teachers! Anyone who is looking to publish content on Skillshare must comply with our content guidelines and be approved by our team in order to publish their first class. This article will guide you through everything you need to know about how the process works. When you’re ready to submit your application, you can do so here: Teach on Skillshare.

Please note that we are currently accepting applications in the English language only.

In this article:

  • How the Sign Up Process Works
  • Wondering What to Teach?

How the Sign Up Process Works

Prospective teachers can access our sign up form by heading to skillshare.com/teach. The form requires you to categorize your content, provide an outline of your class, share an example of your teaching and your work, and agree to our Terms & Content Guidelines. If you’re new to Skillshare, be sure to check out our article, How Learning on Skillshare Works, for a high-level overview of how Skillshare classes are structured.

Your application must be approved through our submission process and any class you publish to Skillshare must follow our Class Quality Guidelines to remain live on the platform.

If you’re approved to teach, you’ll receive access to our class upload tool that will enable you to publish a class at any time, as well as our Skillshare Teacher Welcome Packet that aims to help set you up for success and provide insight into what a great Skillshare class looks like!

Wondering What to Teach?

Before completing our sign up form, we recommend you check our In-Demand topic list, for a list of categories we’re currently accepting from new teachers. Head to our article, What categories and topics can I teach on Skillshare?, to learn what we currently accept, and check out the article Skillshare Content Guidelines to see what is not permitted.

Still need help?

If you have any questions, please do not hesitate to email us at teach@skillshare.com. We’re happy to help!

How do I update my Skillshare profile as a teacher?

With the launch of Creator Hub, teachers now have a dedicated way to manage their profile. Customizing your teacher profile establishes trust with your audience and highlights your creative expertise.

In this article:

  • About Teacher Profiles
  • Edit Your Basic Information
  • Add a Video Intro for 1-on-1 Hosting
  • Archive Profile Page Content
  • Make Your Teacher Profile Private If you’re a student looking to update your account details or display name, head to this article instead.

About Teacher Profiles

As a teacher, your profile appears in two key places:

  • Your teacher profile page: This is your public-facing profile that showcases your classes, published projects, and anything else to establish your expertise.
  • Your 1-on-1 booking profile: If you offer 1-on-1 Sessions, the basic information from your profile (photo, name, short bio, and social links) is shared here as well, so students can learn more about you before booking. In other words, your basic profile info is updated in your Creator Hub settings and reflected across both surfaces — whether or not you’re hosting 1-on-1s. You can further customize your teacher profile page directly on the page itself.

Edit Your Basic Information

Your basic information includes:

  • Profile photo
  • First and last name
  • Title
  • Short bio (specific to 1-on-1 Sessions only)
  • Website and/or social media links This information is reflected across your teacher profile and the profile on your 1-on-1 Sessions booking page, if you’re hosting.

To update your basic info:

  • Log in to Skillshare with your teaching credentials and navigate to Creator Hub.
  • In the left-hand menu, select Edit Profile under Settings.
  • Update the information in each field — changes are saved automatically.

Add a Video Intro for 1-on-1 Hosting

If you’re offering 1-on-1 Sessions, you can upload a short intro video (in vertical format) instead of a profile photo on your 1-on-1 Sessions booking page. It’s a great way to introduce yourself and give students a preview of what to expect if they book with you.

  • Log in to Skillshare with your teaching credentials and navigate to Creator Hub.
  • In the left-hand menu, select Edit Profile in the general settings section at the bottom.
  • Select Upload Profile Video.
  • Upload a video shot in vertical format (max file size: 1024MB).

Edit Your Teacher Profile Page Content

Your teacher profile page is where you can showcase your work, highlight specific classes, or share more about your teaching style.

To edit your profile content:

  • Log in to Skillshare with your teaching credentials and navigate to Creator Hub.

  • In the left-hand menu, select View Profile at the top of the page.

  • On your profile page, click Edit Content. To add a new section:

  • Scroll down to the bottom of your profile and click +Add Section.

  • Select the type of section you’d like to add: Content, Projects, Teaching, or Discussions. Add an optional title.

  • To rearrange sections, use the arrow keys beside the section title to change its position on your profile.

  • To delete a section, click the trash can icon beside the title. To edit content within a section:

  • For Content sections, click under the section title to type or paste text. Highlight text to bring up the formatting menu. You can also embed images, videos (YouTube/Vimeo), or external links using the icons at the bottom.

  • For Projects, Teaching, and Discussions sections, content cards are added automatically to your profile page when you publish that content. You can drag and drop them within their section to reorder them.

  • Click Save and Exit at the top of the page to save your changes. A few tips for profile sections:

  • New content appears at the bottom of each section by default. To highlight something new (like a class or project), drag it to the top of its section.

  • Project and Teaching sections display up to 6 cards at a time. If you add more, a See More button will appear so students can scroll through the rest.

  • You can create multiple sections for projects, classes, or discussions — and rename them however you like to better organize your profile.

Archive Profile Page Content

Want to clean up your profile without deleting content? You can archive classes, projects, or discussions so they no longer appear on your public profile.

To archive content:

  • In Edit Content mode, scroll to the bottom of the section where the item appears.
  • Drag and drop the item into their respective Archive area at the bottom of the page.
  • Click Save and Exit to confirm your changes. You can restore archived items anytime by dragging them back into their original section.

If you set a project to private, it will automatically appear in your Project Archive.

Make Your Teacher Profile Private

  • Log in to Skillshare with your teaching credentials and navigate to Creator Hub.
  • In the left-hand menu, select Settings.
  • On the Account Settings tab, beside Profile Settings, click Edit.
  • Check Make my profile private.
  • Click Save changes. Setting your profile to private will also hide your classes, 1-on-1s, and digital products in Skillshare search.

How Learning on Skillshare Works

Skillshare classes are authentic, actionable, and designed to help students bring what they learn into the real world. Focusing on in-demand creative and entrepreneurial skills, popular topics include design, illustration, writing, photography, productivity, freelancing, and much more.

On Skillshare, students watch classes at their own pace, create projects to practice what they’ve learned, and interact with each other from all around the world. As a teacher, you get to facilitate this unique learning experience with the three pillars of Skillshare’s pedagogy, or approach to teaching.

Focused and Digestible

Skillshare classes, on average, include 20-60 minutes of pre-recorded video content broken down into a series of short, 2- to 8-minute videos. Rather than teaching a broad, sweeping skill set, the most successful classes hone in on one specific project, concept, or skill. At Skillshare we call this the class’s value proposition, and it’s more than just what students will learn. The value proposition should also outline who the class is for, what they will get out of it, and how the skills learned in the class are applicable in a wider context.

Project-Based

All classes on Skillshare include a project that gives students an opportunity to practice what they have learned. Creating and sharing projects in each class’s Project Gallery, and giving and receiving feedback, are at the core of engaged learning on our platform.

A successful Skillshare class is not just a step-by-step tutorial leading to a final project. Instead, the class should empower students with tools and techniques to complete the project as well as strategies to apply them to their own work.

Community-Centered

Skillshare is different from other online learning sites. Our open platform means anyone can teach from anywhere in the world. We are also committed to ensuring teachers and students alike have a personal and authentic experience and feel connected to this global, creative community.

In addition to a Project Gallery, each class has a discussion board that offers a way for teachers and students to share ideas and ask questions. These peer-to-peer and teacher-to-student interactions are at the heart of the Skillshare community. Here, open, personal, and real connection makes everyone feel included and welcome, and makes sharing feel safe.

Go Further

Curious about Skillshare or how the site works? Review these student support articles:

  • What is Skillshare?
  • How does Skillshare work?

In-Demand Topics

Looking for your next great class idea? This article highlights what Skillshare members are actively searching for, where we see opportunity and how you can use top-searched topics to help your content get discovered.

    • Challenge Classes and Bite-Sized Learning

How to Use This Data

Teachers frequently ask us: What kind of content are students looking for? This article is to help you answer that question, and to make sure your next class aligns with your passions and student demand.

We’ve pulled together insights based on search trends and engagement data across Skillshare, highlighting:

  • Topics members are actively searching for

  • Insights from student interviews and/or class structures teachers tend to see success with

  • Common search topics (keywords) to help optimize your content for discovery in search There are a few ways you can put this data into action:

  • Find your next class topic. Start with what excites you within your area of expertise, then use this list to narrow in on high-interest areas or under-served niches that match this.

  • Leverage for discovery. Already have a topic in mind? Use the topics as keywords here to inform your class title and description, so it’s easier for students to find.

  • Expand your offerings. If you’re planning a 1-on-1 Session or digital product, these topics can help you identify what members might need support with or tools for. Check back often! We update this page quarterly to reflect what members are searching for most.

What’s In Demand Right Now

These topics are trending based on search interest and class engagement. If you already offer classes, 1-on-1s or digital products in one of these categories, consider building on your existing content. If you’re exploring new ideas, use this information to spark inspiration.

  • Our top 10 searched topics on Skillshare are very consistently: Procreate, Blender, Watercolor, drawing, illustration, graphic design, After Effects, photography, Canva, and Figma.
  • Additional popular searches include: Photoshop, Adobe Illustrator, Notion, Unreal Engine, Youtube, Gouache and Crochet.
  • Overall, we see the most consistent demand and continued opportunity for classes in the following categories: Art & Illustration, Design, Creative Career, Film & Video, Photography, Animation & 3D, and AI & Innovation. Want more details on topics by category? Check out our Top Search Topics by Category below.
  • As Skillshare continues to focus on the creative disciplines where learners are most engaged, we are currently reducing our investment in the following categories: Development, Home & Lifestyle, Writing & Publishing, Personal Development, Marketing & Business, Music & Audio, and Productivity. While many classes in these categories remain available on the platform and continue to serve engaged audiences, we are prioritizing growth and new class development in the categories listed above. While other categories see less overall traffic and are promoted at a smaller scale, they still serve engaged, passionate audiences. If you’re teaching in one of these areas, we encourage you to keep publishing! Even if your topic is more niche, there’s real value in building strong connections with your students and offering classes that serve their specific interests.

High Opportunity Areas

These are topics and class formats where we tend to see demand and hear an interest in from students. Consider how you can incorporate these insights into your class!

Challenge Classes and Bite-Sized Learning

We often hear from students that they have limited time in a day to dedicate to Skillshare and love when they can take a class in digestible ‘chunks’. In that vein, challenge classes — classes that guide students through a sequence of small, achievable projects over a defined time period — continue to perform well on Skillshare. These classes boost student engagement and project uploads, which are strong indicators of long-term retention and creative growth.

We encourage teachers to keep experimenting with this format as a way to help students build consistent habits and see progress over time. Think of it as a learning opportunity + community motivator, all in one!

While Art & Illustration topics, especially watercolor and drawing, tend to be a natural fit for this format, teachers have also seen success with journaling or creative prompt classes. Any class idea that offers a series of quick, shareable projects could make a great challenge class.

Ready to launch your own challenge class?

  • Read our recommendations on how to structure a challenge class so it meets our guidelines.
  • Check out our blog article for best practices for challenge classes and some popular examples.
Clarity Meets Personality

One thing we hear again and again is how much students value connecting with Skillshare teachers. This is part of what makes Skillshare unique: the ability to learn from someone whose approach resonates with you, who brings their own perspective, and who’s teaching something you’re genuinely excited to explore. At the same time, many students arrive with a specific goal in mind and are looking for clear guidance on where to start - and where to go next.

We encourage you to offer both. Set expectations upfront by clearly outlining what students will gain from your class, while also infusing your teaching with your personal story. How did you learn this skill? What sparked your own learning journey? And once students finish the class and complete their project, what should they do next?

Classes focused on AI for Creatives

Aligned with our AI Principles, we know Skillshare can be a meaningful destination for creators and creative people to learn and explore how to use AI effectively and ethically without replacing what makes creativity so powerful: the human behind it.

Keep in mind: you don’t need to be an AI expert to teach in this area. AI is ever-evolving and we’re all explorers here! If you’re using AI to streamline your creative process or grow your business, there’s likely an audience eager to learn from your experience, even in a short class. Just be sure to go beyond the hype: members are looking for practical, thoughtful guidance they can apply to their own work.

Since AI is non-stop and ever-changing, we continue to be interested in showcasing how you’re exploring AI as a creative now and the results you’re seeing with it. When it comes to AI, we’re all learning together. Think about:

We’re interested in classes that showcase:

  • how you’re using different tools (e.g. Nano Banana, chatGPT),

  • …for different applications (e.g. image generation, workflow simplification)…

  • …across a multitude of categories (e.g. Design, Film & Video, Art & Illustration) While this is not an exhaustive list and we’re interested in all the ways you’re exploring this topic, here are some examples of areas we see based on popular tooling and where we’re already seeing search demand on Skillshare:

  • Canva’s AI tools, including Magic Studio and Canva AI

  • Notion AI, such as the Chat Assistant, One-click Skills, and Research Mode

  • AI tools for video production, like Descript, CapCut, and Runway

  • AI features across Adobe’s suite of products There is definitely an opportunity for short classes here and we encourage you to think about that format!

What is a Staff Pick?

The Staff Pick badge is a way for the Skillshare team to highlight exceptional classes that go above and beyond to provide students with a valuable, engaging experience. Our Curation Team selects Staff Picks based on quality, topical relevance to the Skillshare audience, and editorial criteria related to Skillshare’s pedagogy and brand identity. Every Staff Pick is an excellent class, but not all excellent classes are Staff Picks. You’ll be notified if your class has been selected!

While there’s no specific formula, here are eight principles that our Curation Team keeps in mind when selecting Staff Picks:

Demystifying

  • The class is insightful and accurate — not superficial.
  • The teacher has subject matter expertise and brings their unique perspective to the class topic.
  • A variety of information is presented (e.g. stats, tools, tips, tricks).
  • Narration emphasizes why over what.

Personal

  • The class is engaging, authentic, and connects with students.
  • The teacher speaks to students with warmth and respect, has an inviting demeanor, and uses references to personal experiences and/or examples.

Actionable

  • The teacher situates insights in a wider context. Students are empowered with strategies, not a step-by-step tutorial.
  • The class emphasizes a technique or framework to empower students.
  • Lessons include examples, demonstrations, and case studies.
  • There is a clear and engaging project prompt.

Well-Organized

  • The class contains clear signals and sequences, and the pace matches the level.
  • The class includes clear lesson titles.

Polished

  • The class makes a great first impression.
  • The title and class description are both accurate and compelling.
  • Class copy is free from typographical errors.
  • Links are clean for easy user navigation.
  • The narration is organized and concise, and students can take confidence that the teacher has invested effort and is a trusted guide.

Clear Value Proposition

  • The class includes clear titles, takeaways, and target audiences.
  • A clear class title, description, and introduction video clarifies what students will learn, why it is valuable in a larger context, and who the class is for.
  • The class delivers what it promises.

Relevant

  • The class covers a topic that is relevant to a wider industry or audience conversation.
  • The class focuses on the details that are relevant to the field of focus.
  • The class features an up-to-date aesthetic and consistent design throughout the content, presentation, and resources.

Compelling

  • The class is visually-dynamic and inspiring.

  • The class includes regular visual changes.

  • The class has a motivational style.

  • Lessons are cohesive and inspiring. Curious what a Staff Pick looks like? Check out a few below:

  • Watercolor in the Woods: A Beginner’s Guide to Painting with Rosalie Haizlett

  • Socially Responsible Photography: Photographing with Intention with Eva Woolridge

  • Illustration & Creative Expression: Simple Exercises to Unlock Creativity with Ira Marcks

  • Mindful Growth & The Creative Journey with Mimi Chao

  • Illustrated Journaling: 14 Days of Prompts with Dylan Mierzwinski

  • Mixed Media Animation: Blending Stop Motion and Illustration with Kaho Yoshida

  • Animation Principles: Add Playful Personality to Your Animations with Jake Bartlett

  • Video for Instagram: Tell an Engaging Story in Less than a Minute with Hallease Narvaez

  • Photography for Creative Block: Create a Photo Collage with Sophia Emmerich

Who owns my class content?

You own the intellectual property rights for all of the original ideas and techniques you include in your class. Ownership of the video content depends on several factors. If you filmed and edited your class yourself, and did not accept any payment from Skillshare in exchange for your work on the class, you retain full ownership of your videos and may host them outside of Skillshare as long as they are not free. For more details see our Terms of Service and Teacher Rules and Requirements.

Billing & Payments

Can I buy Skillshare memberships in bulk?

Definitely! We’d love to help you with special pricing for your business or organization here.

Can I get a refund for my Skillshare subscription or purchase?

Refunds may be available within a limited time window depending on how your subscription or purchase was made. Here’s how to check your eligibility and request a refund.

You can cancel your Skillshare subscription at any time to avoid a future charge, and keep what you paid for. To learn more about cancelling a subscription, refer to our article How do I cancel my Skillshare subscription?.

Check if your Skillshare subscription is eligible for a refund

Refund eligibility depends on the type of trial or billing period associated with your subscription. If you’re eligible for a refund, contact Skillshare Support to submit your request.

If you started with a 7-day free trial

We offer a free trial so you can explore everything before committing.

If you’re charged after your trial and didn’t mean to continue, we understand—life gets busy.

For 7-day trials, contact Support within 48 hours of the charge for a one-time refund, as long as you haven’t used Skillshare since.

If you started with a 14-day (or longer) free trial

Charges after a 14-day or longer free trial are not eligible for refunds.

Subscription renewals

Skillshare does not offer refunds for subscription renewals.

For annual subscriptions, Skillshare sends a renewal reminder email approximately 30 days before the renewal date. You can cancel your subscription anytime before renewal to avoid future charges.

If you’re in the EU

If you’re located in the European Union, you may have a 14-day cooling-off period under local consumer protection laws. If you start a subscription with a free trial, your trial period counts toward this 14-day period. After this period has passed, refunds are not available.

If you’re in the UK

If you’re located in the United Kingdom, you may have a 14-day cooling-off period under local consumer protection laws.

  • If you start a subscription with a free trial, your trial period counts toward this 14-day period.
  • You may also have a new 14-day cooling-off period in certain cases when your subscription renews, such as for annual plans. After the applicable cooling-off period has passed, refunds are not available.

Refunds for SEPA direct debit payments

If you paid for your Skillshare subscription using SEPA direct debit, you can request a refund within 8 weeks of the initial payment.

Refunds for subscriptions purchased through the mobile app

If your subscription was purchased through the Apple App Store or Google Play, Skillshare cannot cancel, refund, or manage that subscription. Deleting the mobile app does not cancel your subscription.

You must request refunds directly through the app store where the purchase was made.

Helpful resources:

  • How to request a refund for purchases made in Apple’s App Store
  • How to request a refund for purchases made in the Google Play Store If you are on a Skillshare for Teams plan, please refer to Payments and Subscriptions for Team plans for more information. The above policy does not apply to team subscriptions. Refunds for team subscriptions will be governed by the specific contractual arrangement.

Refunds for 1-on-1 Sessions

If you booked a 1-on-1 Session, you can cancel or reschedule up to 4 hours before the session begins.

To cancel or reschedule:

  • Open the confirmation email you received when registering.
  • Scroll to the bottom of the email.
  • Click the cancel or reschedule link. If you want to dispute a session after it has ended, email help@skillshare.com within 48 hours of the scheduled session start time and explain why you are requesting a refund.

Refunds for digital products

Digital products are non-refundable and cannot be returned or canceled.

Refund requests may be reviewed only in extraordinary circumstances, such as fraud or clear misrepresentation.

To request a review:

  • Email help@skillshare.com.
  • Send your request within 48 hours of purchase.
  • Include details explaining why you are requesting a refund.

Important things to know

  • Canceling your subscription stops future billing, but it does not automatically issue a refund.
  • If you’ve already received a refund on your account, additional refund requests will not be approved.
  • After cancellation, you will keep access to Skillshare until the end of the current billing period.
  • Subscriptions purchased through Apple or Google must be managed through the respective app store.

Still need help?

If you have questions about your billing or refund eligibility, our Support team is here to help. Contact Skillshare Support and include the email address associated with your account so we can review your request.

Can I purchase individual classes?

Skillshare classes are not available to be purchased individually.

Skillshare provides a platform where anyone can join to discover, take, or even teach a class. When you join Skillshare, we provide the entire range of classes where you can engage through communities, projects, and more!

Your membership includes access to thousands of classes, and you are able to cancel your membership at any point.

How do I cancel my Skillshare subscription?

You can cancel your Skillshare subscription from your Account settings if you signed up on skillshare.com. If you subscribed through the iOS App Store or Google Play, you’ll cancel directly through that app store.

Cancel your subscription

On desktop
  • Go to Account settings on skillshare.com.
  • Select Membership & Payments from the left menu.
  • Click Cancel membership.
  • Follow the prompts to confirm your cancellation.
On the mobile web browser
  • Open skillshare.com in your mobile browser and sign in.
  • Tap your profile icon.
  • Select Account settings.
  • Tap Membership & Payments.
  • Tap Cancel membership, then follow the prompts.

Cancel a subscription purchased through an app store

If you started your subscription through the iOS App Store or Google Play, the app store manages your billing. That means cancellations must be completed through the store where you subscribed.

  • Google Play: Cancel your subscription through your Google Play account settings.
  • iOS App Store: Cancel your subscription in your Apple ID subscription settings.

What happens after you cancel

Your subscription remains active until the end of your current billing period. During that time, you can continue to:

  • Watch classes

  • Update your class projects

  • Participate in class discussions Once the billing period ends, you’ll lose access to:

  • Skillshare classes

  • Downloadable materials shared by teachers

Special cases

Some subscriptions follow a different cancellation process.

Free trials started on skillshare.com

Cancel through Account settings on skillshare.com before the trial ends.

Skillshare for Teams accounts

Cancellation terms depend on your organization’s agreement. Contact your organization’s administrator or reach out to teamhelp@skillshare.com for support.

Tips to know

  • Canceling your subscription does not immediately remove access. You can continue learning until your billing period ends.
  • If you subscribed through Apple or Google, Skillshare cannot cancel the subscription for you.
  • You can rejoin Skillshare anytime to continue learning and creating.

Still need help?

If something isn’t working the way you expect, our Support team is here to help. Reach out through the Help Center and we’ll take a look with you.

How do I purchase a Skillshare membership?

Create a Skillshare account to purchase a membership. When you start your membership, you’ll have unlimited access to all Skillshare classes, additional features such as offline viewing, and your membership will support the work of teachers you love. You are able to cancel at any point in your membership.

To learn more about membership pricing in your region, create an account. If you’re an existing member and logged into your account, visit your Membership & Payments page to see your current pricing.

Accepted forms of payment

There are several ways to enroll in a Skillshare membership:

  • Visa
  • MasterCard
  • American Express
  • Discover
  • Apple Pay
  • Google Pay (on Android devices)
  • SEPA
  • PayPal You can also subscribe for a Skillshare membership through the Google Play store or the Apple app store. These options will be subject to payment options they allow.

We do not currently accept payments via iDEAL, MAESTRO, or prepaid cards. Payment methods are subject to geographic location.

How do I redeem a discount code?

In this guide, we’ll show you how to redeem a discount code when signing up for a Skillshare membership. To follow this guide, you’ll need a discount code.

How to Redeem

To redeem your discount code for a Skillshare membership:

  • Click on your avatar icon, go to Account Settings
  • Select Membership & Payments
  • Depending on what phase you are in, click Visit the membership page or the link to start your trial
  • On the next page, click Enter Gift Card or Discount Code
  • Enter your discount code, ensure the plan, and payment details are correct, then click Start membership

How to Redeem on a Paid Membership

If you are on a paid membership and want to apply an applicable discount code to an upcoming renewal:

  • When logged into your account, go to Membership & Payments
  • Click Enter Discount Code
  • Enter your discount code into the text field
  • Click Apply If you only see a date your membership is set to expire, this is likely because the membership was cancelled. You can resume your membership here.

Important Notes

  • Discount codes do not apply to future recurring payments.
  • You can apply one valid offer code.
  • It isn’t possible to combine multiple offer codes.
  • The Enter Gift Card or Discount Code field may not appear at checkout if another discount code has been automatically applied.

How do I update my payment method?

This guide covers changing and removing your payment method if you paid with a credit card.

Changing your payment method

You can update your payment method to pay for a Skillshare membership anytime. By proceeding, you will be sent to our payment provider, Stripe, and agree to the Stripe Customer Portal Terms of Service.

  • Log in to your Skillshare account
  • Go to Account Settings and enter Membership & Payments
  • Click the link under Payment Settings to update your payment method; this will pre-fill the email address tied to your account
    • Note - the email address here must be the same email as the one you used to sign up for Skillshare. You can check the email address on your account on the Email Address page
      • If you don’t receive the email, be sure to look in your spam or junk folders
  • Review your email inbox for an email from “Skillshare, Inc.”
  • Follow the prompts in the billing portal to update your payment method

Removing your payment method

You can only remove your payment method if your membership has fully expired.

If you paid through a credit card, you can remove your billing information by following the same steps (mentioned above) to access the billing portal. Once logged in, remove your payment method by:

  • Clicking the x icon on the right-hand side

How do I view my billing history and download my receipts?

You can access the billing history and download the receipts of your Skillshare account at any time. This will depend on how you originally subscribed to Skillshare.

Credit Card

  • Click “the payment settings portal” link

  • Check your email to access your portal link

Tax FAQs

Skillshare collects and processes local tax or VAT with each purchase. Below are some questions you may have regarding how we will collect and process taxes:

Why does Skillshare collect tax on purchases?

Skillshare is required by law to collect applicable transaction taxes, including VAT (Value Added Tax), GST (Goods & Services Tax), or other types of sales taxes, which may vary depending on your local, state, and country jurisdiction.

Which purchases will be taxed?

Depending on your location, prices shown on our website may or may not include sales tax or value-added tax. Tax and VAT will be collected on all transactions, including initial subscription purchases, subscription renewals or for any other one-off purchases on our website. Please contact us to submit a tax exemption certificate, if your purchase is exempted for tax purposes.

How much tax will be collected?

Skillshare determines a member’s tax jurisdiction and applicable tax rate at the time of purchase, based on a number of factors, including billing and account information. These amounts can vary depending on your tax jurisdiction and may change over time with local tax requirements.

After any Skillshare purchase, you will receive an email receipt which includes the cost of your membership and any tax paid identified separately. If you have any questions about your tax rate after receiving your receipt, please contact us at help@skillshare.com.

Where can I see a copy of my receipt?

You can access the billing history of your Skillshare account at any time. For more information, go here.

Disclaimer

Skillshare does not provide tax, legal, accounting, or other professional advice or services. Skillshare’s services are not intended to provide, and should not be relied on for, such advice or services. You should consult your own tax, legal, accounting, or other professional advisors for this type of advice or service. Skillshare reserves the right to update its policy to comply with changes in rules or regulations.

Unexpected charges from Skillshare

If you have an unexpected charge from us, it can be one of the following:

  • A $1 pre-authorization charge we’ve sent to your bank to confirm payment information.
  • A renewal charge for a membership that was never cancelled. This guide will help steer you in identifying unexpected charges and, if needed, contacting us for assistance.

Why was I charged from Skillshare?

Skillshare charges will typically appear on your statement as “HELP.SKILLSHARE.COM” or “Skillshare, Inc.” Follow these steps to find details about an unexpected Skillshare charge:

  • Log into your Skillshare account.
  • Go to Account Settings > Membership & Payments to see upcoming renewals for your account. Not sure if you have a Skillshare account? You can try resetting your login details. If you don’t recognize the membership, or you don’t have a Skillshare account, contact us.

Pre-authorization charges

We typically submit a pre-authorization charge to your bank when you:

  • Add or update your payment method on file - the pre-authorized amount is typically $0.00 or $1.00, depending on your bank. This is a temporary charge and will be removed before it settles on your account.
  • Making the initial purchase of a membership - if you find two identical charges in your transactions, we recommend contacting your bank to see if one of the charges is a pre-authorization.

You can start learning on Skillshare with a free trial. After that, a paid subscription gives you unlimited access to thousands of classes—so you can learn at your own pace across desktop and the mobile app (iOS & Android).

Skillshare offers flexible subscription options so you can learn in a way that works for you.

  • Paid subscription: Continue learning with unlimited access to thousands of classes and features like offline viewing, subtitles, transcripts, and more.
  • Regional pricing: Subscription costs may vary depending on your location. To see current pricing for your region, visit the pricing page.

Still need help?

If something doesn’t look right or you have questions about your subscription, our Support team is here to help.

What happens if my credit card expires?

We’ll send you an email letting you know that your credit card failed so that you can update your payment settings.

To change your credit card on file, head to your Membership & Payments page in your Account Settings. You’ll see an option to edit your credit card under Payment Methods. Our system will automatically re-attempt to charge your credit card 7 days after the first payment attempt. If your payment information has yet to be updated, we’ll then temporarily cancel your membership until you’ve had a chance to update it.

What information will I receive about my membership?

Skillshare offers a 7-day free trial to all customers and at times a 30-day free trial for certain promotions. Any customer who signs up for a free trial can cancel at any time during the free trial period to not be charged.

Check out our guide for some recommendations to help ensure that you’ll receive our emails successfully.

Trial Confirmation Email

When you sign up for a free trial, you will receive a trial confirmation email with information about your subscription, such as the date and amount of your upcoming charge. If your free trial is longer than 7 days, you will also receive a reminder email 7 days prior to the trial ending, sharing the same information about your upcoming charge.

Subscription Receipt Email

Once the free trial has ended, we will charge the payment method you provided and send a receipt to your email. The receipt details information on your recent charge and your next renewal, and provides a link to our Help Center if you need to read up on how to cancel your subscription or to contact our Customer Support team.

Renewal Reminder Emails

Skillshare memberships are set to automatically renew upon signing up. Customers can cancel at any time during their membership to avoid a renewal charge. 30 days before an annual membership renews, we’ll send a renewal reminder email to the email address on file.

When will I be charged for my Skillshare membership?

If you subscribed for a Skillshare membership, you will be charged once your trial period ends. You can see your next charge date in your Membership & Payments page.

Our trial offering is a great opportunity for new members to enjoy what Skillshare offers prior to becoming a member. Once your trial period ends, it will automatically transition into a paid subscription, unless you’ve cancelled beforehand. You will be charged after your trial is completed.

If you do not wish to become a paid member after your trial, you can cancel your membership by visiting the Membership & Payments under your Account Settings.

Skillshare is not able to cancel, refund, or manage memberships purchased through the App Store or Google Play Store. If you subscribed to Skillshare from our iOS or Android app, please contact the respective app store directly with questions about billing, cancellations, or refunds. See our guide for more information.

Content Notification Processes

What’s covered in this article:

  • Reporting Alleged Illegal Content Under the Digital Services Act (EEA Only)
  • Reporting Alleged Copyright Infringement (US Only)
  • Reporting Other Alleged Intellectual Property Infringement (Non-EEA Only)
  • How to Contact Us

REPORTING ALLEGED ILLEGAL CONTENT UNDER THE DIGITAL SERVICES ACT (EEA Only)

In accordance with the Digital Service Act (“DSA”), we have specified below the procedure that you must follow to provide us with notice of alleged illegal content, as well as the actions that we will take in response, and your right to appeal.

Procedure for Reporting Alleged Illegal Content

Summary: To report allegedly illegal content to us, you should send us an email to the address below, explaining why you believe the content to be illegal, where the content can be found, how we can contact you, and a statement that what you’re saying is accurate.

If you believe that content residing on or accessible through the Service (as defined in our Terms of Service) constitutes “illegal content,” as defined by the Digital Services Act, please send a notice using this form or via the contact information listed below that includes the following information (hereinafter, a “DSA Notice”):

  • A substantiated explanation of the reasons why you believe the content in question to be illegal content (a substantiated explanation could include documentary evidence demonstrating, for example, your ownership of contested content, or screenshots of images or discussions you believe to constitute “illegal content” under the DSA);
  • A clear indication of the exact location of that content, such as the exact URL or URLs where the content can be found;
  • Your contact information, including physical address and email address, or if you are submitting the DSA Notice on behalf of another individual/entity, the contact information of that individual/entity;
  • A statement that confirms your bona fide belief that the information and allegations contained in your notice are both accurate and complete.
Action Taken After the Receipt of a Proper DSA Notice

Summary: Once we receive your notice, we’ll review and either remove the content or determine it’s fine to leave up.

Upon receipt of a proper DSA Notice, we will act on the DSA Notice in a timely, diligent, non-arbitrary, and objective manner by either (1) removing the alleged illegal content from our platform, or (2) determining that the noticed content is not illegal and therefore allowing it to remain on our platform. Where appropriate, we may also suspend or terminate the account of an offending user.

Communications with the Noticing Individual

Summary: When you submit a notice to us, we’ll confirm to you that we received it. Later on, we’ll let you know what we did in response to your notice.

Upon receipt of a proper DSA Notice, we will provide a confirmation of receipt to the individual or entity who submitted the DSA Notice (hereinafter, the “Noticing Individual”).

Following any actions taken on the noticed content, or any determinations not to act, we will inform the Noticing Individual of the decision we made and the action we took, if any. This communication will also provide information about the possibility of appeal with respect to our decision. If any automated means were used for our decision-making or processing, that information will be included as well.

Communications with the Impacted User

Summary: If we take action on content or a user on our platform, we’ll let the affected user know, so long as it’s not related to spam.

A user of the Service may be impacted because of actions taken as a result of a DSA Notice. These impacts could include:

  • A restriction on the visibility of content posted or provided by the Impacted User, including the removal of that content, disabling access to the content, or demoting the content;

  • Suspension, termination, or other restrictions of monetary payments;

  • Suspension or termination of the Impacted User’s account. Except where the content at issue involves high-volume commercial content or spam, we will inform all users for whom the DSA applies who are so impacted (hereinafter, an “Impacted User”), whether stemming from a DSA Notice or otherwise, with a clear and specific statement of reasons. This statement will contain:

  • Information on whether our decision entails any of the impacts listed above;

  • The facts and circumstances that we relied on to make that decision, including, where applicable:

    • whether any actions taken were based on the receipt of a DSA Notice;
    • the legal ground we relied on and why it applied, where that was the cause of the content’s removal;
    • The terms and conditions, if any, the content violated, where that was the cause of the content’s removal

  • Information about the possibility of appeal

Procedure for Appealing a Decision or Action

Summary: You can appeal a decision we made (within 6 months) by emailing the address below and specifying the decision you’re appealing and the reason why you think we should reconsider.

The Noticing Individual and/or the Impacted User may appeal a decision or action we took regarding any of the following:

  • A restriction on the visibility of content posted or provided by the Impacted User, including the removal of that content, disabling access to the content, or demoting the content;

  • Suspension, termination, or other restrictions of monetary payments;

  • Suspension or termination of the provision of our services, in whole or in part;

  • Suspension or termination of the Impacted User’s account. Such appeals are permitted only within 6 months of the date we provide notice of our decision or action. To appeal, the Noticing Individual and/or the Impacted User must send a message to appeals@skillshare.com providing the following information:

  • The decision or action being appealed, including any relevant communications received related to the same;

  • The reason why our decision or action should be reversed or reconsidered, including any information relevant to the same. We will provide a decision concerning the appeal without undue delay. Please note that out-of-court dispute settlement services may also be available to Noticing Individuals or Impacted Users.

REPORTING ALLEGED COPYRIGHT INFRINGEMENT (US Only)

In accordance with the Digital Millennium Copyright Act (“DMCA”), we have adopted the following policy toward copyright infringement. We reserve the right to (1) block access to or remove material that we believe in good faith to be copyrighted material that has been illegally copied and distributed by any of our advertisers, affiliates, content providers, members, or users, and (2) remove and discontinue service to repeat offenders.

Procedure for Reporting Alleged Copyright Infringements

Summary: To report content you believe is infringing your copyright, you should send us an email to the address below that includes: (1) where the allegedly infringing content can be found, (2) information about the content you think is being infringed, (3) how we can contact you, (4) a statement that what you’re saying is accurate, and (5) a signature.

If you believe that material or content residing on or accessible through the Service infringes your copyright (or the copyright of someone whom you are authorized to act on behalf of), please send a notice of copyright infringement containing the following information to Skillshare’s Designated Agent to Receive Notification of Claimed Infringement (our “Designated Agent,” whose contact details are listed below):

  • A physical or electronic signature of a person authorized to act on behalf of the owner of the copyright that has been allegedly infringed;
  • Identification of works or materials being infringed;
  • Identification of the material that is claimed to be infringing, including information regarding the location of the infringing materials that the copyright owner seeks to have removed, with sufficient detail so that Skillshare is capable of finding and verifying its existence;
  • Your contact information, including physical address and email address, or if you are submitting the notice on behalf of another individual/entity, the contact information of that individual/entity;
  • A statement that you have a good faith belief that the material identified as infringing is not authorized by the copyright owner, its agent, or the law; and
  • A statement made under penalty of perjury that the information provided is accurate and that you are authorized to make the complaint on behalf of the copyright owner.
Once Proper Bona Fide Infringement Notification is Received by the Designated Agent

Summary: When we receive a notice from you, we’ll look into it and we may remove the content at issue, as well as notify the user who posted it. We may also terminate that user’s account if they’re a repeat offender.

Upon receipt of a proper notice of copyright infringement, we reserve the right to:

  • Remove or disable access to the infringing material;
  • Notify the user who is accused of infringement that we have removed or disabled access to the applicable material; and
  • Terminate such user’s access to the Service if he or she is a repeat offender.
Procedure to Supply a Counter-Notice to the Designated Agent

Summary: If the user who posted the content at issue believes they have the right to post it, they can notify us by sending an email to the address below identifying the content at issue and its location, providing their contact information and consent to jurisdiction in federal court, stating that the user has a good faith belief that the content at issue isn’t infringing, and providing a signature.

If the user who posted the content believes that the material that was removed (or to which access was disabled) is not infringing, or the user believes that they have the right to post and use such material from the copyright owner, the copyright owner’s agent, or, pursuant to the law, the user may send us a counter-notice containing the following information:

  • A physical or electronic signature of the user;
  • Identification of the material that has been removed or to which access has been disabled and the location at which the material appeared before it was removed or disabled;
  • A statement that the user has a good faith belief that the material was removed or disabled as a result of mistake or misidentification of the material; and
  • The user’s name, address, telephone number, and, if available, email address, and a statement that such person or entity consents to the jurisdiction of the Federal Court for the judicial district in which the user’s address is located, or, if the user’s address is located outside the United States, for any judicial district in which Skillshare is located, and that such person or entity will accept service of process from the person who provided notification of the alleged infringement. If a counter-notice is received by the Designated Agent, Skillshare may, in its discretion, send a copy of the counter-notice to the original complaining party informing that person that Skillshare may replace the removed material or cease disabling it in 10 business days. Unless the copyright owner files an action seeking a court order against the user accused of committing infringement, the removed material will be replaced or access to it restored in 10 to 14 business days after receipt of the counter-notice.

REPORTING OTHER ALLEGED INTELLECTUAL PROPERTY INFRINGEMENT (Non-EEA Only)

Summary: If you believe content on our platform is violating your intellectual property rights (if you live in the US, not including copyright), you can let us know by sending an email to the address below and explaining why you believe the content violates your intellectual property rights, which intellectual property right is at issue, where the content can be found, and how we can contact you.

If you believe that content residing on or accessible through the Service (as defined in our Terms of Service) is infringing your intellectual property rights (or those of an organization you represent), please send a notice to the contact information below that includes the following information:

  • A substantiated explanation of the reasons why you believe the content in question is infringing your intellectual property;
  • A specification of what intellectual property right you believe to be infringed (e.g., trademark, patent, copyright)
    • NOTE: If you are in the US and wish to report alleged copyright infringement, you must follow the DMCA process set out above
  • A clear indication of the exact location of the content, such as the exact URL or URLs where the content can be found;
  • Your contact information, including physical address and email address, or if you are submitting the notice on behalf of another individual/entity, the contact information of that individual/entity.

Continue Your Teaching Journey

Build Your Skillshare Channel

Your first class is launched — congratulations! So what comes next? Turns out the best way to build your presence and success on Skillshare is to build your channel and regularly publish new classes to the platform.

In this article:

  • The Impact of Building Your Channel
  • Choosing Your Next Class Topic
  • Go Further Learn tips for building your presence and growing your audience on Skillshare through stories from Top Teachers Amandine Thomas and Fred Trevino. From Teach on Skillshare: Plan, Publish, and Promote an Engaging Class.

The Impact of Building Your Channel

At Skillshare, it’s possible to teach thousands, even tens of thousands, of students around the world and turn teaching into a substantial part of your monthly income. But in order to build the kind of community that can sustain you on Skillshare, you need to keep at it. Building a channel of complementary classes can help you gain momentum on the platform and build a sustainable audience. We recommend publishing a new class once every 2-6 months so that each of your classes is able to reach maximum engagement within the Skillshare community.

With each new class you publish, you will:

  • Engage your existing students and attract new ones.
  • Build a following around your brand as a teacher and creative.
  • Compound your monthly revenue. Multiple classes means more minutes watched per month and more income in your pocket.
  • Get better at teaching! Practice makes perfect: the more you plan, film, and edit classes, the better your classes will be.

Choosing Your Next Class Topic

Sometimes the hardest part of teaching a second (or third) class is coming up with that next great topic. Here are some strategies to help you hone in on that next class idea:

Analyze What’s Working

Look at your existing classes, social media posts, and anywhere else you share your work. What topics or techniques have received the most engagement? Lean into topics that already resonate with your audience to engage your existing following and attract new students.

Asking your followers, both on and off of Skillshare, is a great way to discover your next class topic. You can use the Discussions feature on your profile to email all of your followers and ask for feedback. Or try using Instagram polls or Google Forms to find out what your followers are interested in learning next.

Conduct Research

Hit the books! Spend some time researching emerging trends or skills in your content area. What new topics or tools are emerging within your content area? What skills are your students, followers, or peers lacking?

Don’t Worry About Duplication

When looking for ideas on what to teach next, some teachers can be discouraged if their topic is already being offered on Skillshare. Don’t fret! Every teacher on Skillshare brings their own unique perspective to a topic, and there’s plenty of room for more diverse approaches and teaching styles within the community.

Create Classes That Integrate

Each of your classes should stand alone so any new student can join in, but try to find ways to connect new classes to what you’re already teaching on Skillshare. By referencing your other classes in each new class that you publish, you can inspire students to take other classes with you while building skills that easily integrate with one another.

Top Teacher Dandan Liu organizes her classes by type on her profile, but she goes the extra mile by including “course maps:” infographics that she designed to help her students structure their learning around specific skill sets in filmmaking.

Top Teacher Marc Barnacle uses similar titling across his “Learn Guitar” classes so that students know that the classes are related.

Establish Your Expertise

It may seem counterintuitive, but teaching on a wide variety of topics can actually work against you when it comes to building and maintaining a following on Skillshare. When students associate you with a particular area of expertise or category, they’re more likely to come back to you when they’re ready to learn more.

If you are interested in teaching across disciplines, it’s a good idea to establish your expertise in one area with a few related classes first before transitioning to a new one. Then, you might consider teaching a class that bridges those two areas of expertise to ease your transition into the new topic area.

Go Further

  • Choose Your Class Topic: This Help Center article is geared toward choosing an idea for your first class but provides a good review of strategies for subsequent classes too!
  • Create Complementary Classes to Build an Engaged Audience: This post on the Skillshare Blog offers concrete tips and examples on how to build a set of complementary classes on Skillshare.

Establish Your Teacher Brand & Grow Your Following

Beyond consistently creating classes, growing your audience means showing up and establishing who you are: being clear about what you teach, sharing who you are as a teacher, and actively engaging with the Skillshare community so students recognize your voice and want to keep learning from you. After all, the goal isn’t just to get students into a single class - the goal is to keep them curious and coming back for your whole catalog.

In this article:

  • Define Your Vision
  • Optimize your Profile
  • Build a Catalog
  • Grow Your Following
  • Promote your Channel
  • Go Further

Define Your Vision

To help you think about your long-term vision for teaching, consider the following questions:

  • Why are you equipped to teach this topic? Showcasing your credentials, experience, and your ‘why’ for teaching establishes trust with potential students and makes them want to learn from you.
  • What’s your unique angle? Don’t just teach “watercolor”; teach “watercolor for magic & whimsey” or “loose, expressive watercolor in 15 minutes.” Specificity is your strength.
  • Define your ideal student. Who are you teaching? Beginners? Professionals pivoting careers? Hobbyist? A blend? The more specific, the easier it becomes to create content that speaks directly to them.
  • How do you want students to feel when taking your classes? What are a few words you want your audience to associate with your classes?
  • What outcome do you promise? Students take classes to achieve something. Will they finish a portfolio piece? Learn a technique? Complete low-pressure projects? State this clearly. Take some time to write 2-3 sentences based on the prompts above. These notes may provide a helpful frame for thinking about future classes and ways to grow your following on Skillshare. Consider how you’ll incorporate these notes into your teacher brand: how you describe your work as a teacher in your bio and elsewhere, in addition to how you market your classes.

Optimize Your Profile

Your profile is your first impression and what prospective students will see when they click on your name. Complete these fields so students know exactly what they’re getting when they land on your page:

  • Teacher Bio: 2-3 sentences explaining what you teach and why. Make it personal and leverage the prompts we shared above: what makes you qualified to teach this topic, and what can students expect?.
  • Profile Photo: Clear, friendly headshot. This builds trust.
  • Categories and Skills: List the specific skills students will gain. These help SEO surface your classes.
  • Highlight popular classes: Think of how other platforms showcase their best stuff first, then rabbit hole you. Surface classes that students should start with and/or you think best establish your credibility as a Teacher. Looking for some inspiration? Check out Build Your Brand Through Your Teacher Profile.

Build a Catalog, Not Just One Class

Each new class brings returning students back to your profile, where they discover your existing classes. Students who take one are exponentially more likely to take your next one.

Publish sustainably: One class every 3-6 months. This gives each class time to gain traction while giving you space to improve.

Bridge your classes: In each class, reference your other offerings so students know what’s next.

Think beyond a single launch. Having more than one class on your profile helps establish your credibility with new prospective students who land on your profile (and gives returning students something new to come back for). New classes give you more opportunities to surface in search while also creating another reason for students to share your work with others.

Ensure your Classes are Primed for Discovery

  • Class Title: Use keywords students search for. “Loose Watercolor Florals” beats “Watercolor Painting.”
  • Class Description: Lead with benefits. “You’ll paint five finished florals and learn to work fast and loose.”
  • Filters & Searchable Attributes: Use all available tags with specific software and materials. Unsure where to start with SEO? Check out Optimizing for SEO.

Grow Your Following: Introduce Yourself & Keep Students Engaged

Consistent engagement builds the following that sustains your growth. Any student on Skillshare can follow you by visiting your teacher profile and clicking the Follow button. Your following is your built-in audience for new classes. Every time you publish a new class, your followers will be notified automatically.

*Note: You need to have at least 50 followers before you can begin earning from your minutes watches on the platform.

Engagement Actions

There are many ways you can engage on Skillshare! Both with your existing students and the platform at large. Use the ideas below to continue to grow your following and meet new people! And don’t forget, there are a million unique ways to engage with your creative community, don’t shy away from thinking outside the box!

Invite Students to Follow You

  • Encourage students to follow you in the class intro or outro to stay updated on new classes, resources, and contests. Keep the mention brief to avoid sounding too promotional. Stay Active in the Classroom

  • Post your own Project in the Project Gallery. This is a nice way to break the ice and make the space look lively.

  • Encourage students who participate in the classroom: respond to their comments in a constructive and positive way, like and comment on their projects, and ask for feedback via reviews. Engage in the Creative Feed (daily)

  • Like and comment on student work: Leave specific, thoughtful feedback

  • Post your own work-in-progress to show you’re still learning

  • Follow fellow teachers and students who are teaching and learning in your discipline.

  • Highlight in-person or virtual events you’re hosting or participating in

  • Tease new classes or content coming out Take a class!

  • Watching a class, uploading a project, and getting familiar with Skillshare as a student is a great way to naturally ingrain yourself in the ecosystem and get people to start recognizing your name and style Post General Discussions (monthly)

  • Announce new classes and ask what they want to learn next

  • Share student wins: When a student completes a project, create a discussion celebrating them. Feature their work. This motivates students and shows others what’s possible.

  • Share your creative process and breakthroughs

  • Request feedback: “What skills feel stuck for you?”

  • Offer exclusive perks: early access, free resources, giveaways 💡Discussions average a 45% email open rate. When you post an update, nearly half your followers actually see it. To learn more about using this feature, refer to the section “Using General Discussion with your Followers” in the article Facilitate Discussions.

And if the thought of starting a discussion from scratch makes you a little nervous, take inspiration from fellow teachers who are already sparking conversation:

A general discussion from Top Teacher Krzysztof Kowalski, where he shares an incoming new class with his followers.

A general discussion from Top Teacher Rose Nene, where she shares a new class, and the story behind it, with her followers.

Promote Your Class!

Marketing your class involves making it visible and motivating people to enroll, treating it as an ongoing conversation focused on reaching your specific audience, and making the promotion feel like storytelling. Success relies on planning ahead, setting simple goals like getting project uploads or thoughtful reviews, and consistently promoting the class on platforms you already use. It is crucial to make your class discoverable by editing the dedicated class page and using your referral link for promotion. The most effective content feels fresh and authentic, often repurposing existing class clips, sharing behind-the-scenes moments, or making posts interactive to boost engagement.

Go Further

Building sustainably on Skillshare isn’t about launching a new class every week; it’s about showing up consistently and making students feel like you’re creating for them. When they feel seen, they take your classes, comment, and come back for the next one.
